```python
import urllib.request
import urllib.error
import html
import re
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from typing import Optional


class _BoilerplateStripper(HTMLParser):
    """
    A streaming HTML parser that removes boilerplate elements (scripts, styles,
    nav, header, footer, aside, ads) and accumulates visible text content.
    Applies heuristics to prefer high-density text blocks.
    """

    # Tags whose entire subtree we skip (content + children)
    SKIP_TAGS = {
        "script", "style", "noscript", "iframe", "svg", "canvas",
        "nav", "header", "footer", "aside", "form", "button",
        "select", "option", "input", "textarea", "figure",
        "figcaption", "menu", "menuitem", "dialog",
    }

    # Attributes that strongly suggest boilerplate / low-value regions
    BOILERPLATE_PATTERNS = re.compile(
        r"(nav|navigation|sidebar|side-bar|side_bar|footer|header|"
        r"menu|advertisement|advert|\bad\b|banner|cookie|popup|"
        r"modal|overlay|social|share|related|comment|widget|promo|"
        r"subscribe|newsletter|breadcrumb|pagination|toolbar|utility)",
        re.IGNORECASE,
    )

    # Block-level tags that act as natural paragraph breaks
    BLOCK_TAGS = {
        "p", "div", "section", "article", "main", "blockquote",
        "h1", "h2", "h3", "h4", "h5", "h6",
        "ul", "ol", "li", "dl", "dt", "dd",
        "table", "tr", "td", "th", "pre", "hr", "br",
    }

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True)
        self._skip_depth = 0       # depth inside a skipped subtree
        self._boilerplate_depth = 0  # depth inside a boilerplate region
        self._chunks: list[str] = []

    def _is_boilerplate_attrs(self, attrs: list) -> bool:
        for name, value in attrs:
            if name in ("id", "class", "role", "aria-label") and value:
                if self.BOILERPLATE_PATTERNS.search(value):
                    return True
        return False

    def handle_starttag(self, tag: str, attrs: list):
        tag = tag.lower()
        if self._skip_depth > 0:
            self._skip_depth += 1
            return
        if tag in self.SKIP_TAGS:
            self._skip_depth += 1
            return
        if self._boilerplate_depth > 0:
            self._boilerplate_depth += 1
            return
        if self._is_boilerplate_attrs(attrs):
            self._boilerplate_depth += 1
            return
        if tag in self.BLOCK_TAGS:
            self._chunks.append("\n")

    def handle_endtag(self, tag: str):
        tag = tag.lower()
        if self._skip_depth > 0:
            self._skip_depth -= 1
            return
        if self._boilerplate_depth > 0:
            self._boilerplate_depth -= 1
            return
        if tag in self.BLOCK_TAGS:
            self._chunks.append("\n")

    def handle_data(self, data: str):
        if self._skip_depth > 0 or self._boilerplate_depth > 0:
            return
        self._chunks.append(data)

    def get_text(self) -> str:
        raw = "".join(self._chunks)
        # Collapse runs of whitespace within lines, then normalise blank lines
        lines = [re.sub(r"[ \t]+", " ", line).strip() for line in raw.splitlines()]
        # Remove lines that are pure noise (very short or look like nav fragments)
        lines = [l for l in lines if len(l) > 1]
        # Collapse 3+ consecutive blank lines into 2
        text = re.sub(r"\n{3,}", "\n\n", "\n".join(lines))
        return text.strip()


def _fetch_html(url: str, timeout: int, user_agent: str) -> str:
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url,
        headers={
            "User-Agent": user_agent,
            "Accept": "text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8",
            "Accept-Language": "en-US,en;q=0.5",
        },
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
        content_type = resp.headers.get_content_type()
        charset = resp.headers.get_content_charset("utf-8") or "utf-8"
        raw_bytes = resp.read()
    return raw_bytes.decode(charset, errors="replace")


def execute(
    url: str,
    timeout: int = 15,
    user_agent: str = "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; ReadabilityBot/1.0)",
    max_chars: Optional[int] = None,
) -> dict:
    """
    Fetch the HTML at *url* and return its main readable text.

    Parameters
    ----------
    url       : The fully-qualified URL to scrape (must start with http/https).
    timeout   : HTTP request timeout in seconds (default 15).
    user_agent: User-Agent header sent with the request.
    max_chars : If set, truncate the extracted text to this many characters.

    Returns
    -------
    A dict with keys:
      - "url"         : the URL that was fetched
      - "text"        : extracted readable text
      - "char_count"  : number of characters in the extracted text
      - "truncated"   : True if max_chars was applied and text was cut
      - "error"       : None on success, or an error message string
    """
    result = {
        "url": url,
        "text": "",
        "char_count": 0,
        "truncated": False,
        "error": None,
    }

    # Basic validation
    if not isinstance(url, str) or not re.match(r"https?://", url, re.IGNORECASE):
        result["error"] = "Invalid URL: must begin with http:// or https://"
        return result

    try:
        html_content = _fetch_html(url, timeout=timeout, user_agent=user_agent)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:
        result["error"] = f"HTTP error {exc.code}: {exc.reason}"
        return result
    except urllib.error.URLError as exc:
        result["error"] = f"URL error: {exc.reason}"
        return result
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
        result["error"] = f"Unexpected fetch error: {exc}"
        return result

    try:
        parser = _BoilerplateStripper()
        parser.feed(html_content)
        text = parser.get_text()
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
        result["error"] = f"Parsing error: {exc}"
        return result

    truncated = False
    if max_chars and len(text) > max_chars:
        text = text[:max_chars]
        truncated = True

    result["text"] = text
    result["char_count"] = len(text)
    result["truncated"] = truncated
    return result

```

## Adversarial Tests Generated:
```python
import pytest
import urllib.error
from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock
from generated_tool import execute


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test 1: Invalid / non-string URLs should return an error, never raise
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad_url", [
    "",                        # empty string
    "ftp://example.com",       # wrong scheme
    "example.com",             # no scheme at all
    "http",                    # incomplete
    None,                      # NoneType
    123,                       # integer
    ["http://example.com"],    # list
    {"url": "http://x.com"},   # dict
    b"http://example.com",     # bytes
])
def test_invalid_url_returns_error_dict(bad_url):
    """Non-http/https or non-string inputs must return an error, not raise."""
    result = execute(bad_url)
    assert isinstance(result, dict), "execute() must always return a dict"
    assert result["error"] is not None, f"Expected an error for input {bad_url!r}"
    assert result["text"] == ""
    assert result["char_count"] == 0
    assert result["truncated"] is False


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test 2: Network errors are caught and surfaced cleanly
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def test_http_error_is_caught():
    """A 404 HTTPError must populate the error field and not propagate."""
    http_err = urllib.error.HTTPError(
        url="http://example.com",
        code=404,
        msg="Not Found",
        hdrs=None,
        fp=None,
    )
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", side_effect=http_err):
        result = execute("http://example.com")
    assert result["error"] is not None
    assert "404" in result["error"]
    assert result["text"] == ""


def test_url_error_is_caught():
    """A URLError (e.g. DNS failure) must be caught and returned as error."""
    url_err = urllib.error.URLError(reason="Name or service not known")
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", side_effect=url_err):
        result = execute("http://no-such-domain.invalid")
    assert result["error"] is not None
    assert result["text"] == ""


def test_timeout_is_caught():
    """A socket timeout (raised as a generic Exception) must not crash execute()."""
    import socket
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", side_effect=socket.timeout("timed out")):
        result = execute("http://example.com", timeout=1)
    assert result["error"] is not None
    assert result["text"] == ""


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test 3: max_chars edge cases
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def test_max_chars_zero_does_not_truncate():
    """
    max_chars=0 is falsy; the code uses `if max_chars`, so 0 must NOT
    trigger truncation.  Verify the contract is consistent.
    """
    html_body = "<html><body><p>" + ("A" * 500) + "</p></body></html>"
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=html_body):
        result = execute("http://example.com", max_chars=0)
    # 0 is falsy -> no truncation path -> truncated must be False
    assert result["truncated"] is False
    assert result["error"] is None


def test_max_chars_exactly_at_boundary():
    """max_chars equal to text length must NOT set truncated=True."""
    content = "Hello World"
    html_body = f"<html><body><p>{content}</p></body></html>"
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=html_body):
        result = execute("http://example.com", max_chars=len(result_text := content))
        # Re-run cleanly
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=html_body):
        r = execute("http://example.com", max_chars=len(content))
    # text <= max_chars, so truncated must be False
    assert r["truncated"] is False
    assert len(r["text"]) <= len(content)


def test_max_chars_truncates_correctly():
    """max_chars smaller than extracted text must truncate and set truncated=True."""
    long_text = "W" * 1000
    html_body = f"<html><body><p>{long_text}</p></body></html>"
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=html_body):
        result = execute("http://example.com", max_chars=50)
    assert result["truncated"] is True
    assert result["char_count"] == 50
    assert len(result["text"]) == 50


def test_max_chars_negative_does_not_truncate():
    """
    Negative max_chars is truthy but len(text) > negative is always True,
    which would slice text to a negative index — returning an empty string.
    Document / catch this footgun: the result must at least not raise.
    """
    html_body = "<html><body><p>Some content here.</p></body></html>"
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=html_body):
        result = execute("http://example.com", max_chars=-1)
    # Must not raise; we just verify it returns a valid dict
    assert isinstance(result, dict)
    assert result["error"] is None
    # If truncation was applied with -1 the text will be empty — flag that
    if result["truncated"]:
        # text[:−1] silently drops the last char; char_count should match text
        assert result["char_count"] == len(result["text"])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test 4: Malicious / pathological HTML that could break the parser
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
@pytest.mark.parametrize("evil_html", [
    # Deeply nested tags
    "<div>" * 5000 + "content" + "</div>" * 5000,
    # Script injection that should be stripped
    "<html><body><script>alert('xss')</script><p>Real text</p></body></html>",
    # Completely empty document
    "",
    # Non-HTML gibberish
    "just plain text no tags at all!!!",
    # Null bytes
    "<html><body><p>hello\x00world</p></body></html>",
    # Broken / unclosed tags
    "<html><body><p>unclosed para<div>nested<p>more",
    # HTML entities only
    "&lt;not a tag&gt;&amp;stuff&quot;",
    # Very large document
    "<p>" + ("word " * 100_000) + "</p>",
])
def test_pathological_html_does_not_raise(evil_html):
    """Parser must never propagate an exception regardless of HTML content."""
    with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=evil_html):
        result = execute("http://example.com")
    assert isinstance(result, dict)
    # Either succeeds or surfaces a clean error — never an unhandled exception
    assert result["char_count"] == len(result["text"])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Test 5: Return-value contract is always honoured
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def test_return_value_always_has_all_keys():
    """Every code path must return a dict with the exact five expected keys."""
    required_keys = {"url", "text", "char_count", "truncated", "error"}

    scenarios = [
        # (url, mock_side_effect_or_return)
        ("not-a-url", None),   # validation failure path
        ("http://example.com", urllib.error.URLError("fail")),  # network failure
        ("http://example.com", "<p>ok</p>"),   # happy path
    ]

    for url, side_effect_or_html in scenarios:
        if side_effect_or_html is None:
            result = execute(url)
        elif isinstance(side_effect_or_html, Exception):
            with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", side_effect=side_effect_or_html):
                result = execute(url)
        else:
            with patch("generated_tool._fetch_html", return_value=side_effect_or_html):
                result = execute(url)

        assert isinstance(result, dict), f"Expected dict for scenario url={url!r}"
        assert required_keys == set(result.keys()), (
            f"Missing or extra keys for url={url!r}: got {set(result.keys())}"
        )
        assert result["url"] == url
        assert isinstance(result["text"], str)
        assert isinstance(result["char_count"], int)
        assert isinstance(result["truncated"], bool)
        assert result["char_count"] == len(result["text"])
```
